# Resumed staged verification: completed checkpoints

The SSProve proof of concept was retired on 2026-09-23. Its source, build targets, CI job and Rocq/SSProve dependencies are no longer maintained or checked. Historical results below describe the checks performed at those checkpoints; the former files remain available in Git at `a6d126c82364413428d0197190b2482ee9af71d2`. Current computational reductions use Lean/VCVio, and their scope and remaining composition obligations are recorded in [the component roadmap](proverif-vcvio-component-closure.md).

The early records below describe historical checkpoints. The bounded authenticated-root completion is HB-111 and the fixed-target evolving-chain completion is HB-112. The user resumed the remaining full proof on 2026-09-23 after SSProve retirement; subsequent interface and global-composition work is active.

The work began by reading the repository instructions, remediation plan, maintained verification analysis, retained Phase-1 module and transcript-fidelity test. Both remotes were fetched, and all twelve existing worktrees were inspected before editing; every worktree was clean. The private remote had advanced to `e5a361289191dbb7bab3675d42d688396044d91a`. Registration M3 and security-kdf were already durably integrated and reviewed there, so the post-registration lane could reconcile without editing the registration worktree. Existing unrelated worktrees and the original retained-tail WIP checkpoint remain preserved.

## ProVerif result and validation

The finite continuation consumes the actual successful sequence-three registration poststate, opens the cached sequence-one frame, consumes only that cached key, rejects its replay before KDF or open, and then accepts a genuine sequence-four frame while retaining sequence two and the original send state. A control changing only cached-key consumption demonstrates replay acceptance. The model and runtime witness preserve the full carried state and context. The isolated original-assignment trace remains separate from M3's replicated completion and accepted outer-ID relabel case.

Semantic reconciliation preserves all 47 post-registration facts, all 27 query hashes and the complete checker-branch hash, the independent 297-case continuation matrix, the earlier independent 409-case matrix, and M3's agreement distinctions. The manifest now has 512 facts and the complete fidelity suite has 3,164 deliberate source/model mutations. CI now includes every one of the 30 Make scenarios, including the previously omitted three registration scenarios.

- `cargo test --locked -p beaconcrypt-core --test proverif_transcript_fidelity`: 16/16 tests.
- Focused post-registration and legacy later-registration ProVerif targets: 27/27 and 18/18 exact classifications.
- `make -C beaconcrypt-core check-generated SHELL=/run/current-system/sw/bin/bash PROVERIF_TIMEOUT=900`: required locked `verify`, all 30 listed scenarios plus two auxiliary result groups, SSProve compilation/assumption/kernel checks, regenerated Lean/ProVerif artifacts, nine panic-coverage regressions, 269 operation certificates and a full 3,168-job Lean build; all pass, no generated drift.
- Separate final `make -C beaconcrypt-core check-inventory SHELL=/run/current-system/sw/bin/bash`: passes after explicit fingerprint and structural review.
- `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked`: 285 tests pass. `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked`, and both no-default-feature role-specific library checks pass; existing unrelated lint warnings remain.
- Complete configured `cargo mutants --workspace --jobs 6`: 1,173 cases in 36 minutes, 816 caught, 357 unviable, zero missed and zero timed out. Configuration and exclusions are unchanged.

## Lean result and validation

The full tail, client and outer identity/signing-writer `OracleComp` equalities preserve private prekey and one-time results, identity and target, actual signatures, adaptive candidate and continuation. The exact ordered log retains all three message/signature pairs. The stopped accepted same-target changed-field event yields one fresh valid weak-EUF-CMA forgery, and the constructed standard VCVio adversary gives a coefficient-one advantage bound with no field guess or collision term.

The query module proves signing upper bound three for the retained tail, its local KEM interpretation, the complete client and the actual forgery reduction. The full tail inherits the selector's explicit logical decapsulation cap `qD`; setup and signing add no logical decapsulation. Successful results have exactly three log entries; aborted computations need not complete all three calls. Underlying ambient/uniform primitive workloads remain in the computation and are not declared free.

- Both directly changed modules pass direct Lean 4.31.0 checks without diagnostics.
- Final serial `lake build`: 3,169 jobs pass.
- Locked `make -C beaconcrypt-core check-generated SHELL=/run/current-system/sw/bin/bash PROVERIF_TIMEOUT=900`: all 29 scenarios plus two auxiliary groups on this separate baseline, SSProve checks, both regenerated extractions, nine panic-coverage regressions, 269 operation certificates and 3,169 Lean jobs pass; no generated drift.
- Separate final inventory check passes with 121 handwritten Lean files. Thirteen guarded axiom reports across the two modules allow only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; forbidden-placeholder and diff checks pass.
- The complete mutation evidence above applies to the exact shared production sources and unchanged mutation configuration, verified by Git comparison on both checkpoints. The ProVerif worktree additionally runs its enlarged fidelity suite; this Lean branch's own aggregate passes its 14 fidelity tests.

## Assumptions and remaining gaps

The structural Lean equalities and fresh-valid extraction assume no cryptographic security conclusion. The algebraic advantage bound uses deterministic verification and a universally quantified runtime map-preservation law; query accounting uses only the selector's explicit resource cap. A small right-hand side depends on the separately stated Ed25519 weak-EUF-CMA contract. The symbolic fixture uses the existing ideal component equations and exact authenticated-prefix equality gate; it adds no primitive-security assumption.

HKDF-SHA-512, ChaCha20-Poly1305, ML-KEM-768, Ed25519, X25519 and BLAKE2b-512 implementations remain outside scope and are treated as perfect implementations satisfying their stated contracts. No primitive internals were proved. Generator-order interchange, related uses and correlations across subsequent phases, source/ideal semantic instantiation, complete oracle/resource simulation, and initialized-chain/KDF secrecy composition remain open. The secret-input KDF work has landed and been reviewed, but this stopped Phase-1 result is not composed into it. The finite ProVerif fixture does not cover arbitrary schedules or all M3 relabel completions; source tripwires and runtime witnesses do not constitute semantic cross-language refinement. Multi-session, reveal, QPT/QROM, asymptotic efficiency, parser/compiler, persistence and physical-erasure boundaries remain explicit.

## Review and environment record

Independent agents reviewed the finite trace, every query hash, the preserved mutation matrices, Make/CI equality, and the complete retained Lean statements and assumptions. A fresh nonauthor reviewed both final Lean modules after earlier reviewers contributed bounded lifting helpers. Shared references and inventory changes stayed with the integration owner.

The locked environment was restored after the previous garbage collection. Final Rust checks used Rust 1.96, Cap'n Proto 1.1.0 and the existing host linker workaround. An initial M3 run timed out under toolchain-rebuild contention; unchanged controls pass in the final aggregate with the explicit 900-second limit. Initial mutation attempts stopped before testing mutants because of host tool selection/build dependencies, and only the complete passing run is counted. A duplicate Lean build caused transient artifact-write errors; the final owner-only serial and locked builds pass. Automatic approval review initially mistook the staged authorized baseline merge for unrelated dirty work; an exact index-versus-`MERGE_HEAD` audit demonstrated that only the nine reviewed Lean files differed from `e5a361289191dbb7bab3675d42d688396044d91a`, and approval then allowed the normal merge commit and push.

All thirteen worktrees were clean after publication, including every unrelated pre-existing worktree. All proof agents completed, and the host process audit found no repository Nix shell, build, Lean, Cargo, ProVerif or Rocq process before cleanup. Logs are retained under `/tmp/beaconcrypt-staged-*`, `/tmp/beaconcrypt-phase1-*` and `/tmp/phase1-writer-*`.

After those checks, `nix-collect-garbage` completed successfully: 5,530 store paths deleted and 18.7 GiB freed. No Nix-backed verification was started after cleanup.

## SSProve proof-of-concept retirement

At the user's request, the SSProve proof of concept is removed after the completed HB-112 checkpoint `a6d126c82364413428d0197190b2482ee9af71d2`. This removes all eighteen proof/configuration/documentation files under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/ssprove`, its standalone composition roadmap, the Make targets and aggregate call, one CI matrix entry, the dedicated Rocq/SSProve packages and `proof-nixpkgs` lock input, and only its inventory categories and theorem checks. The remaining inventory has 923 entries. PL-13, LA-08 and HB-31 through HB-42 stay reserved as historical identifiers; their original source and scope remain available at the checkpoint above. Earlier verification receipts retain their historical results.

Independent review checked that every retained toolchain pin, Lean and ProVerif route, source/model file and inventory gate is unchanged. A mechanical comparison covered 896 Rust, Lean, ProVerif and schema files and found identical bytes. The Make and CI scenario sets still agree on all 32 scenarios; the auxiliary and live-material routes also remain. The integration combines the original source audit, native send/receive and complete owner/role/LIVE compilers, concrete preparation/initializer callbacks, complete-state nonce coupling, and the same-run source sending-input attribution. The [integration analysis](../formal-verification-analysis.md#hb-126-complete-native-record-family-and-source-nonce-event-charge) records the exact interfaces and limitations. The retained source candidate is `3d245601073079002e1e49951f7c3452f029e3e8`; its only proof-file adjustments during integration remove one redundant final blank line from each of `RawCtxSelectedFamilySendEvents.lean` and `RawCtxSourceEventRecorder.lean`. Review reconstructed and approved those exact whitespace changes.

The actual source record client preserves optional plaintext, all role/private/cache fields, routing and AD selection, failed-send advancement, receive rollback, reads and the original public services. It has at most one dynamic component call per record/public attempt and zero per reveal, with the separate private-lookup limits of 51 per receive and one per send. The complete attempted finish has at most sixteen service calls, at most one first receive and at most one selected opening; external callbacks perform no selected opening. With C = max(qLocal,64) + qDecap, D = 34 + qBase + qDecap and A = qAux, the concrete preparation/application, initializer, selector and auxiliary budgets remain C, 32, D and A respectively. These bounds do not erase prefix, known-key, primitive or local computation costs. The exact full-distribution comparison charges the actual nonce-refusal event once and the source event consumer bounds it by ordered repeated visits to the same selected sending input, also with coefficient one. Neither event is asserted small for arbitrary supplied roles.

The full locked `make -C beaconcrypt-core verify` passes on the frozen candidate: 4,482 Lean jobs and all 6,179 guarded axiom targets; the complete configured ProVerif matrix with 32 main scenarios, two auxiliary result groups, five live-material gates and three live-private-application gates; all 53 generated registration cases and 2,444 exact result classifications; the KEM controller gate's nine authentication, eight reachability, six control and four disclosure classifications and five checker tests; and 17/17 transcript-fidelity tests. Dependency preparation, forbidden-placeholder/custom-axiom and root-import gates, nine panic-checker tests and 279 extracted-operation contracts pass. The full Make command used the installed Make executable and `SHELL=/run/current-system/sw/bin/bash` because `/bin/bash` is absent on this host; the maintained Makefile and its gates are unchanged. `LEAN_NUM_THREADS=1` limited the aggregate build while focused checks were temporarily held for memory.

The separate `make -C beaconcrypt-core check-inventory` passes with 1,110 entries and 941 handwritten Lean modules. All 1,086 preceding inventory category/path entries preserve their order; twenty-four Lean paths are appended. Only the prior root importer, inventory checker and trust-boundary hashes change. Each earlier check remains enabled. Rust formatting and whitespace checks pass. Static comparison against `0b4039e0fb2da6b8f48fd7f10e06f39c812a8123` preserves all 36 Rust files, three schemas, 96 existing ProVerif-directory files, 923 prior Lean files other than the root importer, and seven Make/CI files byte-for-byte. Fresh extraction leaves the generated Lean and ProVerif artifacts unchanged. All 49 reviewed integration file hashes still match after the complete run.

Charon reported the existing unsupported Cargo probe flag during Miri sysroot setup, explicitly fell back to the pinned rustc sysroot and completed extraction. No Miri runtime validation is claimed. No Rust, schema, binding, wire-format or primitive contract change is included. No new Rust runtime, binding or mutation campaign is claimed; mutations are omitted under the user's Rust-only instruction. Primitive implementations, snapshots, persistence and restoration remain outside the active proof.

The successful full Make log is `/tmp/beaconcrypt-hb126-full-make.log`, SHA-256 `d0fd868953a947f225fd5eb3cc62312ec4ff29841c160a71795a0480ed76fd97`. The separate receipts are `/tmp/beaconcrypt-hb126-post-make-inventory.log`, `/tmp/beaconcrypt-hb126-modules.json`, `/tmp/beaconcrypt-hb126-independent-wiring-review.json` and `/tmp/beaconcrypt-hb126-after-make-static-audit.json`. Independent review inspected theorems, source continuations, full state, ordinary interfaces, loss/query accounting, documentation limits and inventory preservation. Only this validation record is added after the successful full run; no proof or validation code changes afterward.

The broader goal remains active. Actual installation and attempted-initializer origins, source path/collision probabilities, selected-key/context nonexposure, ordinary authentication/privacy decisions, complete saved Post/Builder continuations, dynamic identity/KEM creation and disclosure scheduling, global selection and the common computational-symbolic event experiment remain consuming obligations. ## Item 8 validation run (2026-10-18)

On 2026-10-18 the owner ran item 8, the final validation matrix of the [completion handoff](handover/completion-2026-10-11/README.md#item-8-the-owners-validation-checklist), on a fresh clone `~/bc-item8` of origin (`ssh://git@git.mschn.fr/amn/beaconcrypt`) at `codex/full-proof-composition` `4aaa1b8558c83383fc86f97d6a160804530686be`, with the submodules initialized at `rooterberg` `85c1ae75` and `wycheproof` `b61843a9`. The machine was the 32 GiB / 16 CPU NixOS host, with no other agent running. Claude wrote this entry from the owner's report and from its own step 7 runs. All reported checks passed. The owner ran the full verification, the Rust, Go and Python checks and the mutation suite; their results below are owner-reported, Claude did not see their full output, and no log of them is cited. Claude ran the two step 7 scans, read only, on the owner's clone.

Full verification (checklist step 5), owner-reported. The owner entered the locked proof shell through a two-file flake copy (`~/bc-flake`, holding only `flake.nix` and `flake.lock`) and ran `make -C beaconcrypt-core verify-in-shell SHELL=bash`, which passed. That target runs, in one invocation, the toolchain check, ProVerif extraction, the legacy ProVerif roster with its prerequisite groups, the free-schedule group, the eleven extension targets, `extract-lean`, `check-lean` (dependency patches, policy, root and panic-freedom gates, then the full `lake --rehash build`) and `check-inventory` (`beaconcrypt-core/Makefile`). It was the first single full `lake build` that covered the item 1 to 7 modules together. The owner reports that the Lean build passed "with a lot of warnings". The output overflowed the terminal buffer and no log was retained, so this entry records no job, theorem, gate or warning counts. The separate transcript-fidelity run (step 4) and inventory run (step 6) were not reported separately; both targets also run inside `verify-in-shell` (`check-proverif` depends on `check-proverif-transcript-fidelity`, and `check-inventory` is its last target).

Rust, Go and Python (checklist steps 2 and 3), owner-reported, all passed, run outside the proof shell: `cargo build --workspace --locked`; `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked`; both role/feature checks (`cargo check -p beaconcrypt --no-default-features --features pqxdh,beacon --lib` and the same with `pqxdh,server`); `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked`; the `gobinds` release build and `go test -race ./...`; `uv run maturin develop --uv` and `uv run pytest tests`; Black, with the exclusion of correction 6 below (64 files unchanged), and `gofmt`. No test counts or Clippy warnings are recorded.

Mutation suite (checklist step 8), owner-reported. The owner's terminal output, verbatim:

```text
[user@nixos:~/bc-item8]$ env -u CARGO_TARGET_DIR cargo mutants --workspace --jobs 12
 WARN --jobs=12 is probably too high and may overload your machine: each job runs a separate `cargo` process, and cargo may internally start many threads and subprocesses; values <= 8 are usually safe
Found 1190 mutants to test
ok       Unmutated baseline in 17s build + 13s test
 INFO Auto-set test timeout to 70s
1190 mutants tested in 16m: 816 caught, 374 unviable
```

The 816 caught and 374 unviable mutants account for all 1,190, so no viable mutant was missed and none timed out.

Forbidden-placeholder and axiom scans (checklist step 7), run by Claude on the owner's clone, read only. Claude used the handoff's two programs: `lean-scan.py` (SHA-256 `61deb5fdff698e45c7fc3180820c214aa4d6f22e6c96ace03f87a040f1248880`) and `axiom-walk-body.lean` (SHA-256 `eb157a8a5c5a8b50f1a7f828d850174b8ab3e66e073903dd469f23b01947f5be`), each byte-identical to the corresponding program text in the handoff's "Step 7 programs". The programs and their outputs are in Claude's machine-local scratch (`/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/completion-audit/step7/`, transient), and the clone's `git status` was clean before and after. The source scan printed `LEAN-SCAN files 1989 hits 0` and exited 0 in 17 s. The exact axiom walk, `AxiomWalk.lean` with 1,989 imports, ran with `lake env lean` inside the proof shell against the full build's oleans, exited 0 in 22 s and printed the following, line for line identical to the handoff's expected block:

```text
AXIOM-WALK roots 95086 reached 121963 unresolved 0
AXIOM-WALK axioms [Classical.choice, Quot.sound, propext]
SERVER-DOMAIN PqxdhDeploymentKemTwoSided.kem_transfer_two_sided true
SERVER-DOMAIN PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile.ideal_compile true
SERVER-DOMAIN PqxdhDeploymentIdealHandoff.ideal_world_view true
SERVER-DOMAIN PqxdhRegistrationJointKemKeyTransfer.allIdeal_loss false
SERVER-DOMAIN PqxdhRegistrationJointKemPostSkeleton.opaqueBranch_shared false
SERVER-DOMAIN PqxdhRegistrationJointBeaconFinishOnce.slotGame_two false
```

Before these lines the walk printed two warnings that the `Hax` and `aeneas` repositories under `.lake/packages` have local changes. The changed files there, 2 in `Hax` and 28 in `aeneas`, are exactly those of `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/patches/hax-lean-4.34.patch` and `patches/aeneas-lean-4.34.patch`, and both patches reverse-apply cleanly (`git apply --reverse --check`), so the local changes are exactly the repository's dependency patches.

Checklist corrections found during this run, applied to the handoff's item 8 section on 2026-10-18:

1. The Rust, Go and Python steps run outside the proof shell. The proof shell is Hax's CI environment with rustc 1.93.0-nightly, while `beaconcrypt` declares `rust-version = "1.96"`.
2. On this NixOS host the system rustc is 1.95. A 1.96.0 toolchain exists under `~/.rustup/toolchains/1.96.0-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` (`rustup` itself is not on `PATH`); its `bin` goes first on `PATH`.
3. That toolchain was installed by nixpkgs' rustup, whose patched `gcc-ld/ld.lld` wrapper points into a garbage-collected store path, so linking fails unless rust-lld is bypassed with `export RUSTFLAGS="-C linker-features=-lld"` (the system `cc` and `ld` are then used; verified).
4. `capnp`, `cargo-mutants` and GNU make are missing from the host `PATH` (`libsodium-sys-stable` builds the vendored libsodium from source with make); they come from `nix --extra-experimental-features 'nix-command flakes' shell nixpkgs#capnproto nixpkgs#cargo-mutants nixpkgs#gnumake`.
5. The Go bindings link from `${SRCDIR}/target/release` (`beaconcrypt.go:8`), so the `gobinds` build must not use `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`: `env -u CARGO_TARGET_DIR cargo build -p beaconcrypt --release --features gobinds && go test -race ./...`. `cargo mutants` also runs without `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, because its parallel jobs would share one target directory.
6. Black runs with `--target-version py311` (`requires-python >= 3.11`; this avoids a spurious Python 3.15 safety-check warning) and also excludes `doc/impl/handover`, whose three archived handover scripts are committed as evidence and are not Black-formatted (CI does not run Black): `uv run black --check --target-version py311 --extend-exclude '/(wycheproof|rooterberg|\.lake|doc/impl/handover)/' .` reported 64 files unchanged.
7. The commands run one at a time, or chained with `&&`: pasting the block continues after a failure and buries the first error.
8. To retain evidence, long steps are piped through `tee`, for example `make -C beaconcrypt-core verify-in-shell SHELL=bash 2>&1 | tee ~/item8-verify.log`.

Not done. The merge basis is the owner's decision and was not taken in this run: central contains `5cbd76e3` (HB-95) and `12d5dfcc`, which `vcvio` and `main` lack. `nix-collect-garbage` (step 9) was not run. This entry records the validation run and changes no proof, model, validation code or production file.

Status mark, 2026-10-18: this item 8 record (branch `codex/item8-record-2026-10-18`, review fix pass `a07d0cc2`) was integrated into central by fast-forward on 2026-10-18 (coordination intent #551, integration #552); a confirming review approved `a07d0cc2` with nits on 2026-10-18; and `origin/vcvio` was fast-forwarded `8330cdef..a07d0cc2` on 2026-10-18 at the owner's request.

## Hax 0.4.2 upgrade (2026-10-18)

At the owner's request of 2026-10-18, Claude upgraded Hax to the latest stable release on branch `codex/hax-0.4.2`, in a fresh clone of origin (`ssh://git@git.mschn.fr/amn/beaconcrypt`) based on central `a07d0cc2fce7f54504cd1b1ba18fdcd18fd11949`, with the submodules at `rooterberg` `85c1ae75` and `wycheproof` `b61843a9`. The owner allowed Claude to run the full validation for this upgrade itself; every result below is Claude's own run on the 32 GiB / 16 CPU NixOS host, with no Codex or owner process active (two Claude instances overlapped in the clone; see "Concurrent instances" below). The commits are `b7a20da7` (pins), `19dd0fe7` (proof adaptation), `abc3e02a` (documentation and inventory), `401949f9` (this record), `49d79f80` (run 3 and mutation results), `29ed4c31` (corrections to this record) and the commit that applied the findings of the three final reviews of `29ed4c31` (see "Final reviews" below).

Toolchain. cargo-hax 0.4.2 (release `cargo-hax-v0.4.2`, `5ccf89dd2e996c9f88f2267eaa2a64739c4ca41a`, published 2026-10-01; previously 0.4.0, `f8fe69339b69e48a01b8a6a6bcb2ab5e5c5e424d`) is the latest stable release on GitHub and crates.io. cargo-hax requires the crate's direct `hax-lib` dependency to have its own version, so `hax-lib`, `hax-lib-macros` and `hax-lib-macros-types` moved from 0.4.0 to 0.4.2 (crates.io checksums `fb3bfcfc...`, `6e901099...`, `e053e3a5...`; the published sources equal the release tag). Hax-Lean moved from v0.3.17 (`b49608d5e402577b400569ee989c3f1b69e4aaf3`) to v0.3.30 (`174840bf2f633ae3bae955e08f781b3737ffc0b8`), the `hax-lean-lib` default of cargo-hax 0.4.2. The newer tag v0.3.31 was not taken: it was published after the 0.4.2 release from unreleased upstream work and no Hax release pins it. Unchanged: Aeneas `nightly-2026.09.03-6852e64` (`6852e6474ec508930acba4df79977b3483a10d10`, the Aeneas Lean library that Hax-Lean v0.3.30 itself requires; `hax.toml` keeps it instead of cargo-hax 0.4.2's built-in default `build-183e4f0`), Charon `nightly-2026.09.02`, the proof shell's `rustc 1.93.0-nightly (843f8ce2e 2025-11-07)` and `cargo 1.93.0-nightly (636800288 2025-10-31)`, ProVerif 2.05 (the old and new shells resolve rustc and ProVerif to the same Nix store paths), Lean `v4.34.0`, Mathlib `v4.34.0`, VCVio `v4.34.0`, and every other Lake package. Relocking changed only the `hax` node of `flake.lock`; the Lake manifest changed only the two `Hax` lines (`rev`, `inputRev`). `make check-toolchain` now expects Hax 0.4.2.

Changed control files: `flake.nix`, `flake.lock`, `Cargo.lock` (the three hax-lib entries), `beaconcrypt-core/Cargo.toml`, `hax.toml` (`hax-lean-lib`), `beaconcrypt-core/Makefile` (`EXPECTED_HAX`), `proofs/lean/lakefile.toml`, `proofs/lean/lake-manifest.json`, `proofs/lean/apply-dependency-patches.sh` (configuration hashes, the Hax-Lean revision, the patch and diff hashes) and `proofs/lean/patches/hax-lean-4.34.patch`. The Hax-Lean overlay was re-ported, not rewritten: both target regions are textually identical in v0.3.30, every added and removed line is the same, and only the `CoreModels/RustPrimitives/Funs.lean` hunk moved 27 lines down (a 15-line module header and the new 12-line `slice_copy_within` model above it), with its base blob index. Its sha256 is now `0e00e9a90566e40be3ba86ef091cd07852342743e642da81ff146e9e49976d14` for both the patch and the resulting diff; a trial build of unpatched v0.3.30 during the preparatory survey failed exactly where the overlay intervenes, and the patched library builds under Lean 4.34.0.

Regenerated artifacts: none changed. Fresh `make extract-proverif` and `make extract-lean` with cargo-hax 0.4.2 reproduce `proofs/pro-verif/extraction/lib.pvl` (sha256 `8cb49efa98fd52479a9cfd99c47104d30c33848b085743558885afa83c96f7b5`) and all seven generated Lean files byte-for-byte, so `git diff` is empty and no inventory hash of a generated artifact changed. Why: the ProVerif backend and every phase of its pipeline are unchanged between 0.4.0 and 0.4.2, as are the Rust frontend and beaconcrypt's `hax_lib` macro expansions; the Lean driver changed only a proc-macro host flag that beaconcrypt-core does not use, and Aeneas and Charon are pinned to the same releases. Because every ProVerif input and the ProVerif binary are unchanged, no query verdict and no row of `doc/proverif-source-scope.md` can change. cargo-hax 0.4.2 newly prints that it uses the configured Aeneas and Lean versions instead of the ones it was tested with (both deliberate). The full review is Claude's machine-local `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/hax-upgrade/regen-review.md` (transient).

Proof adaptation. Hax-Lean v0.3.30 defines two `CoreModels` operations that the generated code calls differently, with the same results: `core.option.Option.is_some` is generated from a Rust core model as a match instead of redirecting to Aeneas, and `core.Array.Insts.CoreCloneClone.clone` is an element-wise `array.Array.map` instead of the identity. The first full build with unmodified proofs failed with seven errors in `PanicFreedom/RatchetLoops`, `Refinement/RatchetRestoreAtomic` and `PanicFreedom/Pqxdh`, and blocked every module that imports them (only 140 of the 1,989 project modules were built). Two bridge lemmas fix them without changing any statement: `BeaconcryptCore.core_option_is_some_eq_ok` (end of `PanicFreedom/Control.lean`, by cases, no axioms), which sixteen `simp` sets in eight modules now use instead of unfolding `is_some`, and `BeaconcryptCore.PanicFreedom.core_array_clone_u8_eq_ok` (from Hax-Lean's `array_map_spec`), which `established_peer_clone_ok` now uses instead of `rfl`. The `is_some` lemma was first placed at the end of `Refinement/RatchetControl.lean`; the transcript-fidelity test then failed because `check-exact-far.py` pins that file's hash as reviewed source, so the lemma moved and `RatchetControl.lean` is byte-for-byte unchanged. The two new lemmas and the eleven changed theorems that had no guard received exact `#print axioms` guards (the `is_some` bridge depends on no axioms, the other twelve on exactly `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`). `doc/formal-verification-analysis.md` explains the change in plain English. Ten handwritten Lean files changed, all of them existing central modules: `PanicFreedom/Control`, `PanicFreedom/Effects`, `PanicFreedom/Pqxdh`, `PanicFreedom/RatchetLoops`, `PanicFreedom/RatchetReceive`, `Refinement/RatchetReceiveLoopExact`, `Refinement/RatchetReceiveRollback`, `Refinement/RatchetRestoreAtomic`, `Computational/RawCtxInterpretedReceive` and `Computational/RawCtxReceiveSendFields`. The handwritten-Lean count stays 1,982. Section 3 of coordination protocol v2.1 freezes existing central modules and asks for a request to the other agent before one changes. No request was sent before these proof-only edits (the upgrade was owner-requested and Codex was out of budget), so the central integration intent for this branch has to name the ten modules and the reason.

Validation, Claude's runs (logs in the machine-local, transient `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/hax-upgrade/logs/`). All checks below passed unless stated otherwise.

- `make -C beaconcrypt-core check-toolchain SHELL=bash` in the new proof shell (entered through a two-file flake copy): rustc, cargo and ProVerif as above, Hax 0.4.2.
- `make check-lean` on the adapted tree: dependency preparation (patch and diff hashes), the policy and root gates, the panic-freedom checker (9 checker tests, contracts for 279 extracted operations), then the full `lake --rehash build`: 5,524 jobs, all 1,989 project modules, 0 errors, 19,706 `#guard_msgs` axiom guards (13 new). The build prints 6,156 warning lines, mostly deprecations of `if_pos`, `if_neg`, `if_true` and `if_false`; none is on a changed line. The unchanged Mathlib, VCVio, Aeneas and other packages were copied from the owner's `~/bc-item8` build; Hax-Lean was fetched fresh and every project module was rebuilt.
- The item 8 step 7 programs of the completion handoff, byte-identical copies (`lean-scan.py` `61deb5fd...`, `axiom-walk-body.lean` `eb157a8a...`): `LEAN-SCAN files 1989 hits 0`; `AXIOM-WALK roots 95086` became `roots 95095 reached 122052 unresolved 0`, with axioms exactly `[Classical.choice, Quot.sound, propext]` and the six SERVER-DOMAIN lines unchanged. The first source scan (2026-10-18, 15:33) ran on an intermediate tree in which the `is_some` lemma was still in `Refinement/RatchetControl.lean`. It was rerun on 2026-10-19 at `29ed4c31`, whose Lean files equal those of `19dd0fe7`, with the same output and exit 0 (`step7/lean-scan-29ed4c31.out`). The axiom walk and the enumeration below used the full build of the final Lean tree (the third `make check-lean` run, 2026-10-18, 15:38 to 15:58). The root count grew by nine, from 19 constants that appeared and 10 that disappeared, and the reached count grew by 89 (the new roots included). Apart from the two new lemmas, every root that appeared or disappeared is an auxiliary or equation lemma that Lean generates. The ones that appeared are two auxiliary proofs of `core_array_clone_u8_eq_ok`, the equation lemma of `EstablishedPeer`'s `clone`, four `CoreModels` equation lemmas now generated in project modules (`Array` and `U64` `clone`, `Array.map`, and the second equation of `Option.is_some`), six private match equation and splitter lemmas of `iter_fold_loop.body`, and four `_proof_1_N` auxiliaries of three existing theorems under new numbers. The ones that disappeared are six private match equation and splitter lemmas of `Option.isSome` and the same four auxiliaries under their old numbers. The itemization is the constant fingerprint of the final artifacts review (`/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/hax-upgrade/final-review-artifacts/fp-compare.txt`, transient). An enumeration of every axiom constant in the environment of all 1,989 modules found the same 33 constants as recorded for v0.3.17 in `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/trusted-boundary.md`.
- `coord.sh inv recount` (handwritten Lean 1,982, unchanged), then `coord.sh inv rehash` of the 24 changed rows, then `make check-inventory`: "Formal-verification inventory matches the reviewed boundary".
- Rust, Go and Python, outside the proof shell, with the 1.96.0 toolchain, `RUSTFLAGS='-C linker-features=-lld'` and `nix shell nixpkgs#capnproto nixpkgs#cargo-mutants nixpkgs#gnumake`: `cargo build --workspace --locked`; `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` (310 passed, 0 failed; it compiles `beaconcrypt-core` with the `proverif` feature against hax-lib 0.4.2); both role checks (`pqxdh,beacon` and `pqxdh,server`); `cargo check -p beaconcrypt-core --features proverif --locked`; `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked` (existing warnings only); the `gobinds` release build without `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and `go test -race ./...`; `gofmt`; `uv run maturin develop --uv` and `uv run pytest tests` (32 passed); Black with the corrected exclusion (64 files unchanged). The first `cargo test` run failed only because the `is_some` lemma was then still in the pinned `RatchetControl.lean` (see above); after the move it passed. The whole list was repeated on the record commit `49d79f80` after the mutation suite and passed again (310 tests; `go test` reported its result from Go's test cache).
- ProVerif, run 1 of `make verify-in-shell` at `abc3e02a`, through the coordination wrapper `coord.sh pv`: `check-toolchain`, `extract-proverif` (again byte-identical) and 34 of the 35 `check-proverif` targets passed, the general registration family with 53 cases and 2,444 exact results. `check-proverif-registration-beacon-admission` stopped with ProVerif exit -6, `Fatal error: allocation failure during minor GC`, in its `success` control: the wrapper's 10 GiB address-space cap (`ulimit -v`) was reached (a rerun alone sampled VmPeak equal to the cap). That is a resource abort, not a verdict; every input of the check is byte-identical to central, and the owner's uncapped item 8 run passed it. A rerun of this checker alone with a 16 GiB cap and a 16 GiB lease passed with the recorded classification (eight true results and the explicit unknown, 25 retry tails, one single-preparation edge, three reconstructed sensitivity controls) at a peak of 11,065,440 KiB virtual and 10,801,640 KiB resident memory.
- Run 2 of `make verify-in-shell`, with the 16 GiB cap: `check-toolchain`, `extract-proverif`, all 35 `check-proverif` targets (with the same general registration and Beacon boundary counts), the free-schedule group `check-proverif-fs` (fs-registration 15 exact results, fs-records-s2b 16, fs-records-b2s 18) and `check-proverif-primitive-fidelity` (9 exact results) passed, every result as expected. The run then blocked, without CPU use, in `check-proverif-active-strong-secrecy`: `coord.sh pv` holds `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/proverif.lock` for its whole command, and every process the command starts inherits the locked descriptor, while the checkers of all ten ProVerif extension targets after `check-proverif-primitive-fidelity` take the same lock file themselves with an exclusive `flock` (their `--lock` default, which the Makefile does not override): `check-active-strong-secrecy.py`, `check-pending-registration.py`, `check-registration-provenance.py`, `check-delayed-response.py`, `check-exact-far.py`, `check-record-origin.py`, `check-tasking-origin.py`, `check-application-secrecy.py`, `check-exact-interface-strong.py` and `check-aead-differential.py`. An earlier version of this entry named only five of them, from a truncated search. The first of them therefore waited forever. The main thread stopped the run, which is not a check failure. The same deadlock affects anyone who wraps `make verify`, `make verify-in-shell`, `make verify-proverif`, `make verify-proverif-extensions` or one of these ten checks in `coord.sh pv`. Run 3 therefore used a plain memory lease (`coord.sh lease mem`). That deviates from section 0 of coordination protocol v2.1 ("ProVerif only through `coord.sh pv`"): under a plain memory lease only these ten checkers take `proverif.lock`, while the 35 `check-proverif` targets, `check-proverif-fs` and `check-proverif-primitive-fidelity` run ProVerif without it. That was acceptable here only because the owner had stated on 2026-10-18 that nobody else was on the machine. Where another agent may run ProVerif, these targets need a lasting fix first, for example `coord.sh pv` not passing its locked descriptor to the command, or a Makefile override of the checkers' `--lock` default. Such a fix goes through the protocol's change process (section 2) and has not been made. The pv wrapper's 10 GiB address-space cap is also below the 10.55 GiB peak of `check-proverif-registration-beacon-admission` (run 1 above).
- Run 3 of `make verify-in-shell` at `abc3e02a`, the single complete run of this validation: started on 2026-10-18 at 21:59:54 by Claude under `coord.sh lease mem` (lease 00000700 in the coordination lease history; 16 GiB, no ProVerif lock held by the wrapper, no address-space cap; the protocol deviation described under run 2) and finished on 2026-10-19 at 03:20:53 with exit 0 (log sha256 `31eb1d850ba416439c8c816a1fcc09c15b8c83fb1e89b74f12c19bee02ff4d9a`). Commit `401949f9`, made during the run, changed only this record, which no checker and no inventory row reads. The code is identical across `abc3e02a..29ed4c31`, where only `doc/formal-verification-analysis.md`, `doc/formal-verification.md` and this record changed. Of these, only `doc/formal-verification-analysis.md` is read by a check: the transcript-fidelity test includes it (`include_str!`), and `check-proverif-transcript-fidelity` runs that test. Its change in `49d79f80` was retested by `logs/session2-fidelity-test.log` (the fidelity test on the uncommitted tree equal to `49d79f80`, 30 passed) and by `logs/rust-matrix-final.log` (`49d79f80`, clean tree, 310 tests including those 30). In order: `check-toolchain`; `extract-proverif` (`lib.pvl` unchanged); all 35 `check-proverif` targets with their auxiliary checks (KEM controller 8 reachability, 9 authentication, 6 control and 4 exposed results; the Beacon boundary with eight true results and the explicit unknown, 25 retry tails, one single-preparation edge and three reconstructed sensitivity controls, this time without any cap; general registration 53 cases with 2,444 exact results); `check-proverif-fs` (15, 16 and 18 exact results); `check-proverif-primitive-fidelity` (9 exact results); `check-proverif-active-strong-secrecy`, which completed without blocking; the nine further ProVerif targets (pending registration, AEAD differential, registration provenance, delayed response, exact far, record origin, tasking origin, application secrecy and exact-interface strong secrecy); `extract-lean` (every output `unchanged file`); `check-lean` (5,524 jobs, built successfully); and `check-inventory` ("Formal-verification inventory matches the reviewed boundary"). Every ProVerif result matched its reviewed expectation, so no query verdict changed, and the tree was clean afterwards.
- Complete configured mutation suite after run 3, at `401949f9`, outside the proof shell with the environment of the Rust bullet above and without `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`: `cargo mutants --workspace --jobs 8` (cargo-mutants 27.1.0, configuration and exclusions unchanged) on 2026-10-19 from 03:21:35 to 03:36:32 under a 16 GiB lease, exit 0: 1,190 mutants tested, 816 caught and 374 unviable, with zero missed and zero timed out (unmutated baseline passed). That is the same count and split as the owner's item 8 run above; no Rust source changed, and only the `hax-lib` crates moved in `Cargo.lock`. The mutation evidence is that run's `mutants.out/outcomes.json` (sha256 `bace969fafdb8592fa524c8f37d14ad5ff6b26122b3b1aa36b49b6988dc9e233`, copied to `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/hax-upgrade/mutants-result/`) together with lease 00000701 in the lease history. The head of `logs/mutants.log` is damaged (see "Concurrent instances" below).

Concurrent instances (2026-10-18/19). By a mistake of Claude's main thread, two instances of the same Claude upgrade sub-agent worked in `/home/user/bc-hax` at the same time: at 21:59:24 on 2026-10-18 the main thread resumed the sub-agent while it was still running. The resumed instance started run 3 (lease 00000700, 21:59:54) and the mutation suite (lease 00000701, 03:21:35), ran the final Rust, Go and Python matrix and made commit `29ed4c31`. The original instance made commit `401949f9` and at 03:21:56 requested a duplicate mutation run (lease 00000702, 8 GiB). That request waited for memory budget and was cancelled before it was granted, so it never ran cargo, and it does not appear in the lease history. After the original instance returned at 03:24, its workflow started a second upgrade sub-agent, which made commit `49d79f80` at 03:39 while the resumed instance stayed active until 03:42. The workflow's validation agent then only fetched origin and registered a scratch directory (`val-49d79f80`) before the workflow was stopped; it ran no check. The cancelled request's output redirection truncated `logs/mutants.log`: its head is now lease 00000702's wait message, followed by a run of NUL bytes and then the tail of lease 00000701's output, and `logs/mutants.log.start` reads 03:21:56 instead of 03:21:35. The overlap did not affect any result. No two heavy jobs ran at the same time. The mutation run copied its clean `401949f9` tree by 03:22:12, before the later sub-agent's first edit at 03:28. Run 3 was the only complete `verify-in-shell` run, and lease 00000701 was the only mutation run; its `outcomes.json` agrees with the tail of the log and with the counts above. The times come from the lease history and the agents' session transcripts. The evidence is machine-local and transient, in `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/hax-upgrade/logs/`, `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/hax-upgrade/mutants-result/` and `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/hax-upgrade/step7/`.

Final reviews (2026-10-19). Three independent reviews of `29ed4c31` (artifacts, hygiene and concurrency integrity) found no stop condition: no ProVerif verdict change, no weakened or deleted theorem, no new assumption and no production Rust change. Their findings concerned this entry, wording and pointers in the documentation, coordination process and Claude's machine-local notes. The commit that applied them changed only Markdown: this entry, `doc/formal-verification-analysis.md`, `doc/formal-verification.md`, `doc/impl/vcvio-feasibility.md`, and the pointer to this entry in `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/trusted-boundary.md` together with its inventory row. The transcript-fidelity test includes `doc/formal-verification-analysis.md` and the inventory pins `trusted-boundary.md`, so Claude reran the affected checks on 2026-10-19 on that commit's tree (only this paragraph, which no check reads, was written afterwards). `coord.sh inv rehash` updated the `trusted-boundary.md` row (`0f1fa9bb...` to `b8054f1b...`). In the proof shell, entered through a two-file flake copy, `check-toolchain` (Hax 0.4.2), `check-proverif-transcript-fidelity` (30 passed) and `check-inventory` ("Formal-verification inventory matches the reviewed boundary") exited 0 (lease 00000708, `logs/review-fix-in-shell.log`). Outside the shell, `cargo test -p beaconcrypt-core --all-features --locked --test proverif_transcript_fidelity` with the 1.96.0 toolchain passed 30 tests (lease 00000707, `logs/review-fix-fidelity-test.log`). The source scan was rerun at `29ed4c31` (see the step 7 bullet), and `git diff --check` is clean. No Lean, Rust or ProVerif source and no lock file changed, so no other check was rerun.

Status, 2026-10-19: every validation step for this upgrade passed on the upgraded tree. No ProVerif query verdict changed, no theorem statement was weakened or removed, no assumption or axiom was added, and no production Rust source changed. `nix-collect-garbage` was not run. The branch is not integrated into central. The three final reviews of `29ed4c31` found no stop condition, and their findings were applied as described above; the fix commit itself has not had an independent review.

## Lean warning cleanup (2026-10-19)

At the owner's request of 2026-10-19, Claude removed warning groups 2 and 3 of the full Lean build on branch `codex/lean-warning-cleanup`, in a fresh clone of origin based on central `8f81b003043a0b8ecf4095d22bec3f324b7d1f95`, with the Lake packages and the project build copied read-only from the Hax 0.4.2 validation clone so that no dependency was rebuilt. Group 2 is the Lean 4.34 deprecation of the `if-then-else` lemma names, group 3 the linter findings. Group 1, the deprecations of VCVio's retiring probability interface, is deliberately unchanged: the owner will follow those deprecation warnings on the current VCVio pin in a separate step, without a VCVio bump. The owner approved editing published, frozen modules for this cleanup. Commits: `e3d4eacef27c2c6a1f192fcc599aeb210159611e` (295 Lean files and their 295 inventory rows) and the commit that adds this entry and the plain-English section [Lean warning cleanup: proof-script hygiene (2026-10-19)](../formal-verification-analysis.md#lean-warning-cleanup-proof-script-hygiene-2026-10-19).

What changed. Group 2: 1,318 logged uses of `if_pos`, `if_neg`, `if_true`, `if_false`, `dif_pos` and `dif_neg` were renamed to `ite_eq_left`, `ite_eq_right`, `ite_true`, `ite_false`, `dite_eq_left` and `dite_eq_right` (identical statements; Lean 4.34 proves the old names from the new ones), 34 further logged uses were unused `simp` arguments and were removed, and six uses inside `try` branches that the log cannot show (`PqxdhPhase2GapDhSource`, `PqxdhServerStaticDhSource`) were renamed too. Group 3: 101 unused `simp` arguments removed (the 34 above included); 72 `letI`/`haveI` steps inside theorem proofs became `let`/`have`; six no-op or unreachable tactic steps removed (`PqxdhJointGapDh` two `change`, `RawCtxInterpretedReceive` a `skip` alternative, `RawCtxNativeKdfFamilyBounds` an `all_goals` with no goals, `PqxdhPhase2Selector` and `RawCtxPendingBackendInterpretation` an `rfl`); two `simpa ... using` calls became `simp` (`PqxdhHiddenRoot`); the `2 ^ 512` and `2 ^ 608` calculation steps (`CtxRomAuth`, `RawCtxFullPreimageTester`) rewrite `8 * 64` and `8 * 76` instead of evaluating the powers; `InitializerExplicitReductionBounds.toKdfBounds` and the private `RawCtxOwnerLabels.rules` changed from `def` to `theorem`; `recordWf` and `InitializedChainsExplicitReductionBounds.toInitializerBounds` stay `def` (`check-inventory.sh` requires `^def` for them) and the published `ratchet.concrete.ratchet.control.Refines.finish_receive_with_removal_consumed_refines` keeps its name (cited by `check-inventory.sh`, `RatchetCachedPublication` and the documentation); these three switch their lint off with `set_option linter.defProp false in` or `set_option linter.dupNamespace false in` and a comment that gives the reason. No `#guard_msgs` axiom guard changed: no guard line is in the diff, and all 19,706 guards pass in the build below, so no edited theorem's axiom list changed.

Exceptions, left unedited with their warnings. Files whose content a checker pins: `Model/Ratchet.lean` and `Refinement/RatchetControl.lean`, `RatchetRefinement.lean`, `RatchetFuturePublication.lean`, `RatchetReceiveAdmission.lean` (`check-exact-far.py` `SOURCE_SHA256`), every file under `Model/` (`scripts/check_lean_panic_freedom.py` `IDEAL_MODEL_DIGEST`, one sha256 over the whole directory) and seven computational modules (`source/getter-profile.py`, no warning of groups 2 or 3). They keep 112 group 2 warnings in eleven files: `Model/Pqxdh/Theorems.lean` 28, `Acceptance.lean` 18, `Commit.lean` 9, `Instance.lean` 5, `Kdf.lean` 3, `Runs.lean` 2, `Model/Ratchet.lean` 7, `RatchetRefinement.lean` 17, `RatchetControl.lean` 15, `RatchetFuturePublication.lean` 7 and `RatchetReceiveAdmission.lean` 1. A first pass had also edited six `Model/Pqxdh` files; the first `make check-lean` stopped at the panic-freedom gate on the aggregate digest, and those files were restored byte-for-byte. ## VCVio deprecation migration, stage S0 (2026-10-19)

Superseded in part: [amendment A1](#vcvio-deprecation-migration-stage-s0-amendment-a1-2026-10-20) below removed the `=ᵈ` notation, moved the tactic and the certificate and twin commands out of the build and brought the step 7 source scan back to 0 hits, and [the review fixes](#vcvio-deprecation-migration-stage-s0-review-fixes-2026-10-20) corrected the certificate tool and strengthened the inventory pins. This entry remains the record of `41564767`.

At the owner's decision of 2026-10-19 (no VCVio, Lean or Mathlib change; follow the deprecation warnings of the pinned VCVio `98dc1089`; plan [`vcvio-deprecation-migration-plan.md`](vcvio-deprecation-migration-plan.md) with the recommended answers D1 to D5 and D8 to D11, phase B of D6, and D7 as Lean-level checks for this stage), Claude implemented stage S0 on branch `codex/vcvio-migration-S0` in a fresh clone of origin based on central `b8db7926ba1f9145ddfdb8b7d3f3e1202cb4221e`, with the Lake packages copied read-only from the lane 1 clone and the project built in the clone. Commits: `09bc331b` (the five modules, root imports, manifest rows, the handwritten Lean count 1,982 to 1,987 and the name pins of `check-inventory.sh`), `66f4819f` (the proof-only replacements and their 14 rehashed inventory rows) and the commit that adds this entry, the plain-English section [VCVio deprecation migration, stage S0: forward vocabulary and the transitional bridge (2026-10-19)](../formal-verification-analysis.md#vcvio-deprecation-migration-stage-s0-forward-vocabulary-and-the-transitional-bridge-2026-10-19), the trust-boundary section, a copy of the plan and the receipt `proof-receipts/vcvio-deprecations-s0.json`. No statement changed; the stage's restatement allowlist is empty.

What was added. `VCVioForwardEvalDist` (equality in distribution `EvalDistEq`/`=ᵈ` with its algebra, and event laws: 53 theorems), `VCVioForwardOracleComp` (laws of oracle computations and uniform samples, two integral laws of upstream's `ToMathlib`: 27 theorems; it imports the pin module `VCVio.OracleComp.Constructions.SampleableType.NativeMeasure`, whose `SampleableType.prEvent_uniformSample` has upstream's statement), `VCVioForwardProbabilityTheory` (three project lemmas for the Boolean bound, following the deprecation's replacement behind one lemma, D8), `VCVioForwardProgramLogic` (the import of `VCVio.ProgramLogic.Relational.FromUnary`, D9) and `VCVioLegacyBridge` (14 bridge theorems, each under its own `set_option linter.deprecated false in`; the tactic `vcvio_legacy`; the commands `vcvio_legacy_twin` and `vcvio_legacy_certificate`). The 81 upstream-named backports (80 laws and the definition `EvalDistEq`) keep upstream's names in upstream's namespaces; each name is absent from all 874 Lean files of the pinned VCVio package (an exact-name scan, 0 hits). Pin adjustments, documented in the module headers: laws that upstream states for its head constant `prEvent mx` are stated for `𝒟[mx] {True}`, upstream's `[OracleSpec.AnswerMeasure spec]` is the pin's measurable-space and `IsMeasureSpec` binders, `grind` annotations are not copied, and simplifier attributes are kept only where they close a goal. The primary bridges are stated for `ProbComp`, so that the pin's `DiscreteEvalDistCompatible` link stays inside the bridge; versions for any monad with that link (suffix `_of_compatible`) and for oracle computations over a variable uniform specification (prefix `oracleComp_`) cover the rest. All 97 theorems carry exact `#print axioms` guards: 96 list `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, and `bind_eq_attach_bind` lists `propext` and `Quot.sound`.

What was edited. The fifteen uses of `ProbComp.probOutput_true_le_add_ofReal_boolDistAdvantage` (in `AdaptiveCtxPrefixValidation` two, `CtxComputationalPrivacy` two, `CtxComputationalSecurity` two, `CtxNonceAeadIndDollar`, `CtxNonceAeadIndDollarValidation`, `CtxUnclaimedAcceptance`, `PqxdhProjectionCollisions`, `PqxdhServerAuthPublicationPrivacy` (under an existing suppression that stays for its statement), `RawCtxBadEventMixture`, `RawCtxFullPreimageTester`, `RawCtxScaledBadEventMixture` and `RawCtxSupportedMixture`) now state the bound with `VCVioForward.evalDist_apply_true_le_add_ofReal_boolDistAdvantage` and turn it into the old form with `VCVioLegacy.evalDist_singleton_eq_probOutput`. `PqxdhFirstRecordStopTagged.product_coupling` and `PqxdhServerAuthEntryFactor.relTriple_support` are `relTriple_prod`; the suppression line before the latter is gone, and its docstring now names `relTriple_prod`. Three intermediate forms were replaced during the stage: a `simpa only … using` form of the calculation steps failed where a step names an advantage definition that is only definitionally equal to the Boolean advantage (first build), and the first bridge, stated for any compatible monad, put the pin's compatibility instance into the proof terms of the edited modules (gate G5), and so did an oracle form keyed on `IsUniformSpec`, whose statement fixes the pin's `SPMF` adapter semantics rather than the native semantics of concrete specifications.

Validation, Claude's runs (machine-local, transient logs in `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/vcvio-migration/S0/logs/`). All passed.

- `make check-lean` in the proof shell (two-file flake copy equal to central's) on the final Lean sources, 22:17 to 22:37 (lease 00000754): dependency preparation, the policy and root gates, the panic-freedom checker and `lake --rehash build`, 5,535 jobs (lane 1's 5,524, the five new modules and six newly needed dependency modules), 0 errors, so every axiom guard holds.
- Gate G1, deprecation warnings of the 41 deprecated VCVio declarations at unique positions in project files: 1,359 (lane 1's final build log) to 1,338, exactly the 21 warned sites (14 Boolean-bound, 5 `evalSPMF`, 2 `SPMF`), no increase in any file. Gate G2, lexer scan: 1,753 sites to 1,724 outside the bridge (warned 1,359 to 1,338, suppressed 394 to 386), no new site, suppression lines 183 to 182 outside the bridge. Gate G3, declarations whose statement or definition body uses the deprecated interface: 756 before and after, none in the new modules.
- Gate G4, declaration dump of every `BeaconcryptCore` module (name, kind, universe parameters, type hash, value hash) of the base build (95,092) and of this build (95,248): no type, universe list, module or definition body changed; 158 declarations were added, all in the five new modules; the two removed declarations are the `_simp_1_1` helper lemmas of the two replaced coupling proofs; the proofs that changed are the 17 edited theorems and the instance proof `PqxdhServerAuthControlWitness.instCountableUScalar_beaconcryptCore`, which now finds `Countable (BitVec n)` through `BitVec.instFinite` of the newly imported `ToMathlib.MeasureTheory.DiscreteInstances` (that module also declares `⊤` measurable structures for bit vectors; upstream's sampling modules import it too).
- Gate G5: no forbidden name of plan section 3 is newly referenced outside the bridge. Residual, pairs of a project module and a referenced VCVio constant absent from upstream `afa6593f` (instances excluded; the plan's figure of 2,807 used a definition that the survey files do not preserve, so base and stage are measured here with one script): existing modules 3,221 to 3,193, the new modules 17 by design (the bridge's deprecated constants, `OracleSpec.Fintype`, `IsMeasureSpec` and `IsUniformMeasureSpec` in shim proofs, the real-valued Boolean advantage).
- Forward replay: the 80 backported laws restated with their source text and proved by upstream's constant of the same name applied to the same explicit arguments, against the upstream VCVio `afa6593f` build, plus the definition text of `EvalDistEq` and the `absDiff` Boolean bound as `Measure.apply_true_le_add_boolDist`: all pass; a control with two altered statements fails.
- Certificates (plan section 5.2): the stage certificate file has no entry. A self-test restated eight theorems and two wrapper definitions of stages S1 and S2 in the forward vocabulary (`VCVioFeasibility.ctx_binding_bound_tight`, `RawKeyPartition.sampled_partition`, `StatefulGoodEvent.output_good` and `event_good`, `JointEagerTable.independent_comm_spmf`, `JointTableFreshCandidates.fixed_fresh_guess_bound`, `CtxRomAuth.ctxFreshAliasGame_le_inv`, `PqxdhSecretInputKdf.zeroSalt_key_probability`, `CtxReduction.ctxMisattributionAdvantage`, `CtxAuthClassification.ctxAcceptedFullFreshForgeryProbability`): all ten old statements and bodies were reproduced with the type or value hash and the `pp.all` text of the pre-stage dump of `b8db7926`, one with the second reading of an ambiguous singleton event; a twin of one restatement was generated; a restatement weakened by `+ 1` was rejected.
- Checklist step 7 of [the completion handover](handover/completion-2026-10-11/README.md) on this build: the exact axiom walk printed `AXIOM-WALK roots 95248 reached 130895 unresolved 0` with exactly `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound` and `propext`, and unchanged `SERVER-DOMAIN` lines (the reached set grows mostly through the bridge's metaprogramming code reaching Lean's elaborator); the source scan printed `LEAN-SCAN files 1994 hits 5`, the five lines being `VCVioForwardEvalDist.lean:29` (the `=ᵈ` notation), `VCVioLegacyBridge.lean:142` (`macro "vcvio_legacy"`), `:252` and `:286` (the two command syntaxes) and `:268` (`addDecl` of the twin command). The plan requires this notation and these tools (D2, D4); the scan is the owner's checklist program, not a maintained gate, and its expected output is no longer 0 hits.
- `coord.sh inv recount` (1,982 to 1,987), `coord.sh inv rehash` of the changed rows, then `make check-inventory` in the proof shell on the state of `66f4819f` and again on the final tree: "Formal-verification inventory matches the reviewed boundary".

- Not run: `verify-in-shell` (D7: only after S3, S6 and S9; no ProVerif input changed), `cargo test`, the Go and Python binding tests and the mutation suite (no Rust, Go or Python source changed).

Tooling for the later stages, in the stage scratch directory: a pin-filtered wrapper around a copy of upstream VCVio's `scripts/migrate-native-probability.py` (notation rewrites kept; module remaps, namespace deletions, binder-class rewrites and the `prFail` rewrite disabled; renames limited to the 29 targets that exist at the pin or in the shims), the gate script for G1 to G5, the pre-stage dump and certificate-file generators, the twin rename script driven by the reference probe, and the forward replay generator.
